# FR14 red-team pass 1 — local-bytes verification of recon claims (2026-08-16)

Method: verify the recon agents' HTTP-audit claims against files actually on disk
(unsloth NVFP4 repo small files landed; weights still streaming at ~37 MiB/s).

## Confirmed by bytes

- **Vocab mapping IDENTICAL** 3.6 ↔ 3.8-unsloth: `tokenizer.json` model.vocab exact dict
  equality (248 044 entries) and `vocab.json` sha-identical.
- **Merges IDENTICAL**: raw lists looked fully disjoint (247 587 vs 247 587, zero overlap)
  — that was a tokenizers-library serialization change (`"Ġ t"` strings → `["Ġ","t"]`
  lists). Normalized: identical content AND order.
- ⇒ **The K64 DVK block map (`fr13_dvk_subset_blocks.json` @85dffa58) CARRIES OVER.**
  The recon's "silent accept-rate collapse via id remapping" scenario is ruled out.
  Optional later: refresh block frequencies from a 3.8 trace corpus (drift, not wrongness).
- **NVFP4 quant config as reported**: compressed-tensors `format=mixed-precision`,
  group_0 FP8 (4 target patterns), group_1 NVFP4 g16, 303-entry ignore incl. `re:^mtp.*`,
  static-tensor FP8 KV scheme.

## New findings the recon missed (all real, none blocking)

1. **Chat template materially changed in 3.8**: new system-message merge + a
   `reasoning_effort` mechanism (default `'xhigh'`, values xhigh/medium/low). FR13 serving
   is insulated (launcher pins `docker/chat_templates/qwen3-openai-codex.jinja`), but the
   pinned template is a 3.6-era artifact — template↔model mismatch is now a live quality
   question for exact16. DECISION NEEDED before QC banking: keep pinned template
   (harness-comparable) vs adopt/port the 3.8 template (model-native, likely better solves).
2. **pre_tokenizer regex changed**: 3.6 `[^\r\n\p{L}\p{N}]?[\p{L}\p{M}]+` →
   3.8 `[^\r\n\p{L}\p{N}]?\p{L}+` (combining-marks handling). Alters segmentation only for
   text carrying U+03xx-style combining marks — negligible for SWE/code traffic, but it is
   a genuine behavioral delta, not serialization. Decoder flags also changed
   (add_prefix_space/trim_offsets/use_regex true — decode-side).
3. **+7 added_tokens** (ids 248070–248076: audio/TTS specials) inside the reserved
   248044→248320 padding range. No id reassignment.
4. **unsloth config is a transformers-5.14 re-serialization**: top-level
   `head_dim/num_attention_heads/num_key_value_heads` keys added (values match 3.6 arch
   exactly: 256/24/4), `vision_config.model_type` renamed `qwen3_5` → `qwen3_5_vision`,
   `torch_dtype` → `dtype`. Boot risk to watch: the pinned image's transformers must
   tolerate the 5.x-style config (the official FP8 arm keeps 4.57-style — one more reason
   it is the de-risk arm). Also: recon said unsloth transformers_version 5.16.0.dev0 for
   dbirks; unsloth's own is 5.14.1 — cosmetic, recorded for precision.
5. **merges.txt not yet on disk** for unsloth at check time — indeterminate
   (download in flight); re-verify at download completion. tokenizer_config.json diffs are
   the added audio/vision special-token declarations plus the new chat template.

## Pass 3 (~19:00Z) — self-inflicted download corruption, caught by fail-closed sha check

CONFESSION + DOCTRINE. Sequence: (1) observed 0 B/s over 80 s on the NVFP4 main shard while
the sibling FP8 stream progressed → diagnosed "hung connection"; (2) attempted
`pkill -f 'download unsloth'` in a compound command whose own text matched the pattern →
exit 144, the FR13 pkill-self-match fossil, re-committed verbatim; (3) killed the resumed hf
process and appended the "missing" 281 MB with `curl -C -` into the `.incomplete` file;
(4) size landed exact (22 568 192 096 B) but **sha256 MISMATCHED** the expected
`c473512c…` embedded in the incomplete-file name.

ROOT CAUSE: `hf_xet` is installed — hf 0.34 downloads via the xet protocol, which writes
verified chunks NON-CONTIGUOUSLY into the preallocated file. File size is NOT a contiguity
watermark, "0 B/s by stat()" is NOT proof of a dead transfer (xet verification phases look
identical), and appending with curl at the file's tail corrupts by construction.

RECOVERY: corrupt partial deleted; fresh xet download relaunched (verified-by-construction
chunks); smoke wrapper relaunched after it wrote a spurious DOWNLOAD_INCOMPLETE verdict when
the pkill gap made the downloader vanish mid-poll.

DOCTRINE (additions): (a) never mix transport tools mid-file — one downloader owns a file
from first byte to finalize; (b) file-size deltas are not a progress instrument under xet —
use the downloader's own log; (c) the pkill self-match rule now includes "the killing
command's own argv"; kill by PID, never by pattern, when the pattern appears in your own
command line; (d) the embedded-sha fail-closed check WORKED — it is the only reason the
corrupt shard did not reach the serve path. Always sha-verify any file a second tool touched.

## Pass 4 (~19:45Z) — both downloads frozen; xet CAS implicated; switched to classic HTTP

OBSERVED: both hf processes read 0 bytes over 60 s windows (measured on /proc/<pid>/io rchar
— the correct instrument after pass 3) while an independent curl range request served
3.5 MB/s. The xet cache held a CAS-session dir (https___cas_serv-…): xet transfers ride a
CAS endpoint DIFFERENT from the resolve URL, so my "CDN is fine" probes never tested the
path the downloader actually used. Whether the freeze was the CAS endpoint hanging or a
shared rate-limit window sleeping both processes could not be distinguished from outside;
both fit two-processes-frozen-simultaneously.

ACTION: killed both by PID, deleted all xet-written .incomplete partials (non-contiguous —
untrustworthy for any resume-append), relaunched both with HF_HUB_DISABLE_XET=1. Classic
contiguous HTTP: resumable, sha-verifiable, and every local instrument works on it. After a
brief settle: unsloth 14 MiB/s, fp8 2 MiB/s, both flowing. FP8 completed-file integrity
unaffected (3/3 sha spot-check vs LFS oids: layers-0, layers-20, mtp.safetensors).

DOCTRINE: (e) probe the endpoint the tool actually uses, not the one you know — xet≠resolve;
(f) for unattended big-file fetches prefer the classic path (HF_HUB_DISABLE_XET=1): slower
peak, but contiguous, resumable, and honest to instruments; (g) two independent processes
freezing at the same moment points at shared infrastructure (endpoint, rate bucket, lock),
not per-connection failure — diagnose the shared thing before touching either process.

## Pass 5 (~20:20Z) — FP8-3.8 DROP-IN SERVE: PASS (manual re-verdict over an instrument bug)

The wrapper wrote SMOKE_REQUEST_FAILED, but the evidence says otherwise: engine booted
clean on /models/qwen3.8-27b-fp8 (66 shards, eager, gpu-mem 0.70), /health 200, and R2
returned a full sampled completion — finish_reason=stop, 417 completion tokens, coherent
linked-list-reversal answer, zero degeneration (fp8_smoke_r2.json). R1's file was EMPTY:
the instrument's `curl -m 120` cannot cover 2048 reasoning tokens at eager B1 speed
(~10-20 tok/s) — a false negative by construction. VERDICT: FP8-3.8 drop-in serve PASS;
"model refresh alone breaks nothing" is now measured, not assumed.

INSTRUMENT FIX (both wrappers, committed): R1 max_tokens 2048→512 and -m 120→420;
R2 -m 180→600. NVFP4 wrapper killed and re-armed with the patched instrument BEFORE its
download completes. DOCTRINE (h): a smoke's client timeout must be derived from worst-case
decode speed × token budget, not from optimism — an instrument that can time out on a
healthy server manufactures false failures.

## Pass 6 (~20:50Z) — NVFP4 SERVE: PASS, after two real findings

ATTEMPT 1: BOOT REFUSED — free unified memory 32/117.5 GiB < the 82.3 GiB that
gpu-mem-util 0.70 demands. Cause: page cache from 54 GB of downloads counts against
"free" on GB10 unified memory. DOCTRINE (i): `sudo sysctl vm.drop_caches=3` preflight
before every engine boot; watch `free -g` in every runner preflight (Mark directive:
pay attention to disk and memory).

ATTEMPT 2: FAIL-CLOSED LOAD ERROR — "no parameter named 'lm_head.weight_scale' in
Qwen3_5ForCausalLM". The exact lm_head-scale bug class SGLang recon predicted (#34895),
in its fail-closed vLLM form: this vLLM's qwen3_5 builds lm_head as an UNQUANTIZED
ParallelLMHead (the CT dispatch exists but the model file never routes lm_head through
it), so unsloth's FP8 lm_head tensor set is unloadable. Everything before lm_head —
config parse, mixed-precision config_groups, module construction — was consumed fine.

FIX: checkpoint surgery (lmhead_surgery.py, manifest lmhead_surgery_manifest.json):
dequant lm_head FP8×[248320,1]-scale → BF16 (reproduces the served-3.6 lm_head precision
exactly; DVK drafter slice stays byte-compatible), drop weight_scale, remove lm_head from
group_0 targets + add to ignore. Source-sha verified c473512c… == upstream pin (proves the
re-download intact); new shard bbf67537…/23 839 093 880 B. Original DELETED for disk per
Mark (RECOVERY.json holds the re-download recipe + expected sha). Config backup:
config.json.pre_lmhead_surgery.bak.

ATTEMPT 3: **PASS** (nvfp4_smoke_serve_PASS.json): R1 exact-format answer + correct
arithmetic (17*23=391), R2 3551 chars non-degenerate, 809 completion tokens. The 4-bit
checkpoint SERVES COHERENT TEXT in the pinned production image on GB10 sm_121.
FLOOR NOTE: with BF16 lm_head the head bytes do NOT shrink after all — the floor
re-derivation uses the post-surgery tensor ledger (weights on disk = what streams).

## Pass 9 (~20:45Z) — 13033 diagnosed: NOT a token-ceiling regime. A one-request count gap.

Pass 8's hypothesis ("Qwen3.8 reasons far longer, driving requests into a token-ceiling
regime the FR13-tuned algebra never saw") is **REFUTED by the bracket's own metrics**.
Nothing in this task went near a ceiling:

| engine metric delta, astropy-13033 | value |
|---|---:|
| `max_tokens_count` | 18 |
| `max_tokens_sum` | 589,824 = **18 x 32,768 exactly** |
| `max_tokens_le_20000` (compactions) | **0** |
| `max_tokens_le_10000` | 0 |
| `request_success_stop` | **18** |
| `request_success_length` / `_abort` / `_error` / `_repetition` | 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 |

Every request declared the SAME 32,768 ceiling, every one finished `stop`, and there were
ZERO compaction requests. So the failing clause in `_fixed32_qwen_compaction_metric_evidence`
(contract.py:1174-1183) is not the token-sum term at all — it is the COUNT identity
`normal_request_count + total_compactions != completed`, evaluating as **17 + 0 != 18**.

THE DECISIVE MEASUREMENT: the trace's own usage matches the engine's token counters
**exactly, on both axes** — trace `input_tokens` 595,503 == engine `prompt_tokens` 595,503,
trace `output_tokens` 9,417 == engine `generation_tokens` 9,417, and `result.num_turns` = 17.
The engine therefore admitted and `stop`-terminated an 18th request that contributed
**zero prompt tokens and zero generation tokens**. Both hidden-request classes the contract
already models are zero (`hidden_terminal_model_requests` = 0,
`hidden_compaction_model_requests` = 0).

CONTROL: astropy-12907, same arm, same boot, reconciles PERFECTLY — 10 metric requests vs 10
trace requests, sum 327,680 = 10 x 32,768. So this is not a systematic off-by-one in the
port; it is one anomalous request on one task.

The `max_tokens_*` histogram is admission-side while `prompt_tokens`/`generation_tokens` are
completion-side, so the leading candidate is a **zero-token request at the bracket edge**
(admitted and counted, contributing no tokens) rather than any model-behaviour change. That
is a harness/bracket question, not an NVFP4 question.

NOT FIXED, DELIBERATELY. This validator is the instrument that guarantees measured engine
work corresponds to traced agent work — the property that makes a B1 timing number citable
at all. Relaxing the count identity to absorb an unexplained engine request would delete
exactly that guarantee. Mark's ruling needed on whether a zero-token request is a legal
member of the algebra (and if so, it must be counted explicitly as its own class, never
waved through).

DOCTRINE (n): when a reconciliation instrument fires, read WHICH clause failed before
theorising. Pass 8 attributed this to token ceilings; the same metrics that raised the error
already proved zero ceiling hits and zero compactions. The error STRING named 32768/20000,
but the failing TERM was the request count.

FIRST NVFP4 SPEED OBSERVATIONS (run 1 reduce, **NOT CITABLE** — n_tasks=2, not the canonical
exact4 set): s_per_fwd 0.20389, s_per_fwd_gpu 0.12588, derived_tps 27.28, derived_tps_gpu
44.19, per_request_decode_tps 28.00, accept_per_event 4.563, committed_per_event 5.563,
prefill_frac 0.213, instrument OFF. Recorded as a sighting shot only; the arm never reached
the canonical 4-task set the contract requires.

### Pass 9 CORRECTION (~20:52Z) — cause found: qwen-code `web_fetch`'s hidden side query

My pass-9 "leading candidate" (a zero-token request at the bracket edge) is **WRONG** and is
retracted. The real cause, identified in parallel and now confirmed against this run's
evidence: qwen-code's `web_fetch` is not a retrieval tool — after fetching the URL it issues
a `runSideQuery` model call (purpose "web-fetch", main model, ordinary 32,768 max_tokens) to
extract the answer from the fetched bytes. That call never enters chat history, so it emits
no trace assistant record, but vLLM serves, bills and histograms it.

The tool census settles it, 1:1 on both tasks in the arm:

| task | `web_fetch` uses | engine reqs | trace turns | gap |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| astropy-12907 (control, reconciled) | **0** | 10 | 10 | 0 |
| astropy-13033 (failed) | **1** | 18 | 17 | **1** |

WHERE MY INFERENCE BROKE: I read trace `result.usage` matching the engine exactly
(595,503 / 9,417 on both axes) as proof the extra request carried no tokens. In fact
`result.usage` AGGREGATES every model call qwen-code makes, hidden side queries included —
so it matches the engine precisely while `num_turns` counts only the 17 chat turns. Equal
token totals were never evidence of a zero-token request; they were evidence that the hidden
call's tokens are already inside the aggregate. The count and the usage come from different
denominators in the same file.

DOCTRINE (o): a trace's aggregate usage and its per-turn records answer different questions.
Reconciling an aggregate against an engine counter cannot detect a request that the aggregate
already contains — only the RECORD count can. When two counters agree and a third disagrees,
the agreeing pair may share a denominator rather than corroborate each other.

This makes the finding a HARNESS-COMPLETENESS gap (an unmodelled qwen-code request class),
not an NVFP4 or Qwen3.8 defect at all — and the fail-closed validator was right to stop the
arm. The fix direction (model `web_fetch` as a third hidden-request class, counted
explicitly) is sound; it adds a request class rather than relaxing the identity, which is
exactly the distinction pass 9 asked for.

## Pass 9 (~21:4xZ) — 13033 diagnosed: verdict (a), plus a second, larger finding

VERDICT (a) LATENT VALIDATOR EDGE — but NOT the max-token-clamp mechanism the
hypothesis named. Nothing was clamped. The engine metrics for 13033 are
pristine: 18 completed requests, ALL at max_tokens=32768 (max_tokens_sum delta
589824 = 18x32768 exactly), le_10000=0, le_20000=0, stop=18, abort/error/
length/repetition=0. The constant 20000 never appeared; "32768/20000" is only
the error string naming the two pinned constants.

THE ARITHMETIC THAT FAILED (measured, `_fixed32_qwen_compaction_metric_evidence`):
  normal_request_count (trace) = 17
  total_compactions (le_20000 delta) = 0
  completed (proxy ingress ledger) = 18
  clause 2: 17 + 0 != 18                                    -> FAIL
  clause 3: sum 589824 != 17*32768 + 0*20000 = 557056       -> FAIL by 32768
i.e. the trace was exactly ONE ordinary 32768 request short.

THE MISSING REQUEST. Proxy ledger seq 77-80, task key c456b57b7744: a complete
logical_begin/attempt_begin/attempt_result(200)/logical_complete on route chat,
prompt 1123, completion 601, sitting between the web_fetch turn (34453/141) and
the next agent turn (35160/1409). Fully accountable on OUR OWN instrument; it
simply has no trace assistant record. It is qwen-code 0.19.4's WebFetchTool
side query: `executeDirectFetch` fetches the URL then calls
`runSideQuery({purpose:"web-fetch", model: mainModel, maxAttempts:1})` to
extract the answer from the fetched bytes, and returns only that call's text.
Two terminal displays exist and only two: `Content from {url} processed
successfully.` (emitted on the line AFTER the side query resolves => exactly 1
completed request) and `Error: ...` from the outer catch (=> 0). Trace event 31
is the web_fetch tool_use, event 32 is the success display verbatim.

WHY FR13 NEVER SAW IT: across all 236 banked 3.6 traces, web_fetch appears 0
times and tool_search 0 times. 3.8 called tool_search("select:web_fetch") and
then web_fetch on its SECOND task. Not a length effect — a tool-repertoire
effect. Third hidden-request class after hidden compaction and hidden sub-agent
terminal; the first one that is a TOOL calling the model.

3.8 vs 3.6 REASONING LENGTH (same tasks, 3.6 n=20 traces each):
  12907: thinking 6736 vs 5111 chars/trace (1.32x); output 3908 vs 3061
         tok/trace (1.28x); turns 10 vs 10.5
  13033: thinking 25237 vs 8625 chars/trace (2.93x); output 8816 vs 4756
         tok/trace (1.85x); turns 17 vs 18.1
3.8 reasons 1.3-2.9x longer per trajectory at the SAME turn count. Real, and
worth budgeting for (wall, KV pressure) — but it is NOT what killed the arm.

FIX (committed): `_fixed32_qwen_hidden_web_fetch_requests` counts one ordinary
32768 request per web_fetch whose result is the exact success display for that
invocation's own url; an error display buys none; any other closure, malformed
input, or non-unique owner result fails closed. Not a skip — the engine
histogram and the ingress ledger remain the meters, the trace is only allowed
to NAME the request they already counted. The algebra clause now prints every
measured number (trace normal, le_20000, completed, sum, expected, shortfall)
so the next failure costs one line instead of a diagnosis pass. Verified: the
real 13033 artifacts now reconcile 18/18; all 235 validatable banked 3.6 traces
produce byte-identical model_request_id lists before and after (0 drift).

(n) DOCTRINE: a fail-closed counter must name its measured numbers. This clause
said only "does not reconcile" and cost a full pass.

**BLOCKING FOR ANY FR14 RESOLVE-RATE CLAIM — BENCHMARK CONTAMINATION.** The
same web_fetch that broke the validator fetched
`raw.githubusercontent.com/astropy/astropy/main/astropy/timeseries/core.py` —
the CURRENT upstream source of the exact file under test, which contains the
merged fix — and 13033's produced patch is the upstream gold patch essentially
verbatim, helper `as_scalar_or_list_str` and its body character-for-character.
Instance containers run `--network=host` (runner lines 535/559), so the agent
has full outbound internet; 3.6 never used it, 3.8 does. Serving, acceptance
and throughput measurements are unaffected — the FR14 first-order question is
untouched — but no SWE resolve rate from a network-enabled 3.8 arm is usable.
Disabling web_fetch means changing qwen_system_settings.json, whose sha256 is
pinned in the runtime attestation; that is a campaign-doctrine call, not a
diagnosis call, so it is escalated rather than taken here.

instruments must be capture-aware; an instrument that can perturb its subject's

LATCH FIX LANDED (49d60b862, 271 tests): the gate was fed from stage_fixed32_step's
row delta — EMPTY on every steady-state decode step (safe_end=prior when drafts==31);
generated tokens reach Arctic via finalize_fixed32_step, WHICH HAD NO GATE HOOK. So
decide() ran per-step over THE PROMPT'S FROZEN TAIL — per-step evaluation existed in
form and was a no-op in fact; the 11 latched-ON requests were simply prompts ending

hard latch). FIX: feed from finalize's per-step committed delta — the exact tokens

chunked prefill). Structural invariant added: EVERY function that feeds Arctic must
feed the gate in the same function — fails on the shipped round-5 code.

impossible — proven by test (the copier-runaway keeps the predicate true even under

consecutive gated — orthogonal to the calibrated predicate (8-gram/0.75 and
q1=0.8202 unchanged), cost <=1/33 of the saving, batch-outcome counter, resets on
cold. The principled alternative (exclude matches from the copier's own output) is

new calls would expand to empty string and REFUSE EVERYTHING — and the four

and by (prefix, ORDINAL) — the detector's own first version kept the last region
per prefix and MISSED TWO OF THE FOUR defects it was built for, caught by its own
test. A detector must be able to fail, and its first failure should be in its own
test suite, not in a boot. Full region-AST diffing was COSTED AND REJECTED:
intended fork divergence dominates, the allowlist it would need is an enumerated
roster wearing a different hat. Shape projections generalize; rosters ratchet.

## Pass 153 (2026-08-20) — per-request seeding is REFUSED by the route, not
## ignorable; the gap gets standing tests and three ruled options

THE GAP (01751f7d1, tests-only landing): vLLM supports per-request seeds on our
pinned build end-to-end, and our patcher forwards the generator map verbatim —
but fr13_fixed32_taw_commit's _fr13_fill_uniforms RAISES on any non-empty map
("fixed32 forbids per-request generator maps; the fixed route requires one bulk
device RNG call"). CPU-proven both ways. Injecting seeds at the proxy would
have taken the serve down on request one — NOT a placebo, a crash; the placebo
warning was right to exist and wrong about the failure mode. THE ARCHITECTURAL
CAUSE, named at the line: _fr13_bulk_gen seeds lazily ONCE PER PROCESS from
torch.initial_seed(); every request's uniforms come from one stream, so draws
depend on POSITION — batch composition and every request before you. The
generator's own docstring already admitted it ("the unseeded bulk draw was
never reproducible"). Stochasticity is live (temp 0.6, top_k 20, top_p 0.95,
all_greedy=False). WHAT LANDED: four standing tests that EXECUTE the gap,
including the tripwire that fails when the route gains per-request support —
the moment proxy injection becomes shippable, a test says so. Zero new
failures.

RULINGS ON THE THREE OPTIONS: (A) per-request generators in-route — full
numerical re-qualification, not now. (B) COUNTER-BASED PHILOX addressing keyed
on derived seed + step — the REAL fix (exact replay independent of engine
history, capture-safe, one kernel); still changes uniforms => re-qualification;
GOES TO MARK bundled with the runaway brake as ONE re-qualification window.
(C) FR13_SG_PIN_UNIFORMS=1 — exists today, rng_route=bulk_then_pin, zero
sampling variance, documented selfcheck-only; creates a DIFFERENT deterministic
trajectory (cannot replay banked runs, CAN run controlled A/Bs). RULED:
approved for EXPERIMENT ARMS ONLY — artifact must self-label rng_route, never
QC or promotion evidence; Mark may override. The lane proceeds to the c5
standing gate (corridor [0.40,0.70], per task, flag-not-refuse, windowed
variant per the design doc's F4).

## Pass 155 (2026-08-20) — Mark kills the probe (right call, acted); the basin

PROBE KILLED on Mark's reading, confirmed correct: with the phenomenon

WEB RESEARCH (Mark's ask — is this the model?): YES, substantially. (1)
Thinking-mode runaway/infinite-loop generation is DOCUMENTED across the Qwen3
family — official QwenLM issues for the 3.5 series ("easily fall into infinite
loops during reasoning with the provided sampling parameters", filed on the
Qwen3.8 repo) and community reports; one measurement: 17.4% truncated-thinking
rate on LiveCodeBench with 84% of truncations showing >30% repetition, worse
on harder problems (27.5%). Our serving params (temp 0.6 / top_p 0.95 / top_k
20) ARE the officially recommended settings — the basin exists ON the
recommended configuration, so parameter tuning is not the fix; detection +
brake is. (2) The quantization literature names our failure mode exactly:
quantization error ACCUMULATES over long generation, degrading reasoning
traces as length grows; low-bit reasoning models show "PATH-FINDING FAILURE —
the model does not reach a parseable answer before the trace becomes
repetitive and instead keeps exploring an unproductive path" — a precise
description of the 13236 enumeration (zero tool calls, never commits, explores
a lattice to the cap). Our checkpoint is NVFP4 (4-bit): the aggressive-4bit
workstream should treat elevated basin propensity as a KNOWN COST of the
quantization, priced into its floor derivation, not a stack bug.

HOW FAR "PREDATES": all five banked degenerations are on the Qwen3.8-NVFP4
port (earliest: lever_pair 13033 loop, 2026-08-17; Ch27 both-task loops and
the Cqc16 enumeration, 08-19). NONE banked on the Qwen3.6-fp8 era — but
detection standards were weaker then, so a RETRO C5 SWEEP over pre-port
runroots (the scrapes exist wherever the harness ran) is launched to answer
"is this 3.8/NVFP4-specific" empirically rather than by absence. Its verdict
lands with the sweep report.

## Pass 156 (2026-08-20) — Qwen3.8 has a NATIVE thinking brake and our serve
## never armed it; the brake spec redesigns around it

MARK'S QUESTION ANSWERED BY RESEARCH: Qwen3.8 supports thinking_budget (hard
thinking-token cap, official API default 4000 — the model force-stops thinking
and answers) and reasoning_effort (adaptive low/medium/xhigh), mutually
exclusive. OUR SERVE SETS NEITHER — proven by the incident itself: 33,313
tokens in one thinking block. THE OFFICIAL DEFAULT BUDGET WOULD HAVE CUT THE
ENUMERATION AT ~4K, an 8x earlier stop than the request cap that eventually
fired. The family is documented to overthink by default; deployments are
expected to enforce the budget serving-side; ours did not. The runaway was not
just a model basin — it was a model basin plus an unarmed, ecosystem-standard
brake.

BRAKE SPEC REDESIGNED (dispatched mid-design): first-line = thinking_budget
enforcement at the lowest capture-safe layer (vLLM mechanism vs proxy-side
think-close injection vs harness config — implementability assessment in the
spec), with the default budget chosen FROM DATA (p99 of healthy banked
thinking-block lengths, zero healthy generations clipped) rather than copied
from the API default; second-line = c5 corridor + n-gram screens as flags;
the bespoke in-serve detector-brake demotes to optional; philox-B rides along
as the one re-qualification bundle. Interaction check required: a forced
think-close token must traverse the fixed32 committer/suffix machinery as an
ordinary sampled token. Proposal goes to Mark, not a silent landing.

## Pass 165 (2026-08-20) — SITE 24: the script was edited while bash was
## executing it; the safety landing killed the campaign; the coordinator
## approved the collision

THE DEATH (QC dead at 3 of 15, ~4h in): "line 2978: cho: command not found" —
a command that EXISTS IN NO COMMITTED VERSION of the serve variant. Bash reads
scripts incrementally by byte offset; the 24k-ceiling landing (d390ed5e7)
modified scripts/fr13_bigdenom_swe_serve_variant.sh at 19:50:46 — FOURTEEN
MINUTES AFTER THE QC BOOTED at 19:36 — and when control returned from the
orchestrator 3.5 hours later, bash resumed at a byte offset that no longer
aligned and executed fragment text: "cho", the tail of an echo. The campaign
tore down, the terminal engine-ledger snapshot failed, the container is
preserved holding 96 GiB. Tasks 1-3 completed with full artifacts before the
death (12907, 13033, 13398 — verdicts and boundaries intact; 13398's eval
never invoked).

ATTRIBUTION, ALL OF IT: the landing was correct in content and WRONG IN
TIMING, and the timing was the COORDINATOR'S — the arming was dispatched and
banked mid-drain without checking whether the landing's file set intersected
the running boot's execution closure. The round-21 discipline guarded one
direction only: no boot against dirty files — nothing guarded no LANDING
against a running boot's files. The irony goes on the ledger without
decoration: THE LANDING THAT ARMED THE SAFETY CEILING IS WHAT KILLED THE QC.

THE FIX CLASS, dispatched: (1) DURABLE — vehicles exec a BOOT-TIME SNAPSHOT
of every long-running script in their closure (copy to the runroot, exec the
copy, pin the source sha in provenance) — the running process becomes immune
to tree edits, and the "staged copy is a fork with a birthday" lesson is
inverted correctly: a fork is a hazard when it can DRIFT; a snapshot of a
running process's code is the opposite — it PREVENTS drift mid-execution,
and its birthday is exactly the boot it serves; (2) INTERIM, until snapshots
land — a campaign in flight FREEZES its execution closure: no lane lands
closure files while a boot runs; the derived-closure function (site 22's) is
the freeze list; (3) the RESUME needs an honest 12-task derived subset
(the remainder after 12907/13033/13398/13236) — the sites-19-23 machinery
makes this a routine landing now. bash -n cannot catch this class (cho is
syntactically valid); the snapshot removes the class instead of detecting it.

## Pass 167 (2026-08-20) — site 24 lands: snapshot-exec with the inversion in

after copy, provenance names the exact bytes that ran, SCRIPT_DIR resolution

a fork is a hazard when it can drift from its source OVER TIME (sites 12/14/

DURING one execution and dies with the boot — same mechanism, opposite
lifetime, opposite effect. MUTATION PROOF EXECUTED, not simulated: a victim
